I find myself constantly worrying about my future and even simple daily tasks, which makes me feel incredibly nervous all the time. It feels like this anxiety is always there in the background, even when I'm not directly thinking about the event.\n\nDoctor: Before this stressful event occurred two months ago, did you frequently experience similar periods of persistent, excessive worry about various aspects of your daily life?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I never really felt like this before the event happened. This persistent worry and anxiety only started after that stressful thing occurred two months ago. It feels like a direct reaction to what I went through.

I just know that these anxious feelings definitely became prominent right after that stressful event happened.\n\nDoctor: Since that event, have you found that you are constantly worrying about many different things, such as work, health, or your future, even when you aren't thinking about the event itself?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I find myself constantly worrying about my future and even simple daily tasks, which makes me feel incredibly nervous all the time. It feels like this anxiety is always there in the background, even when I'm not directly thinking about the event.\n\nDoctor: Before this stressful event occurred two months ago, did you frequently experience similar periods of persistent, excessive worry about various aspects of your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, I never really felt like this before the event happened. This persistent worry and anxiety only started after that stressful thing occurred two months ago. It feels like a direct reaction to what I went through.\n\nDoctor: Does this constant worry and anxiety feel overwhelming enough that it significantly interferes with your ability to perform your usual responsibilities at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. The constant worry and nervousness are so overwhelming that I've found it really difficult to focus on my tasks at work or keep up with my responsibilities at home. It feels almost impossible to get through my normal daily routine right now.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, do you feel this level of excessive, uncontrollable worry occurs more days than not, and have you been feeling this way for the majority of the time over these past two months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I would say I feel this way almost every single day. It has been pretty much constant for the entire two months since that stressful event happened."

I am keeping both candidates because the symptoms meet criteria for Adjustment Disorder due to the clear temporal link to the stressor, but the pervasive, generalized nature of the worry warrants consideration of Generalized Anxiety Disorder as the underlying pathology triggered by the event.", "is_final": false}

It has been pretty much constant for the entire two months since that stressful event happened.\n\nDoctor: In addition to your worry, have you been experiencing physical symptoms like muscle tension, feeling easily fatigued, or having difficulty concentrating?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven't really noticed any significant physical symptoms like muscle tension or feeling extremely fatigued. My main issue is really just the constant worry and nervousness that I can't seem to shake off.

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.
